    unknown
    \n
    Unknown. Normally doesn't happen, unless the Lua API is out of sync with\n the C API. (Likewise, it could happen that your script gets reason strings\n that did not exist yet at the time your script was written.)
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file that was being played or attempted to be\n played. This has the same value as the playlist_entry_id field in\n the corresponding start-file event.
    \n
    file_error
    \n
    Set to mpv error string describing the approximate reason why playback\n failed. Unset if no error known. (In Lua scripting, this value was set on\n the error field directly. This is deprecated since mpv 0.33.0. In\n the future, this error", "before the filter name disables the filter by\n default. Everything after this is the normal filter name and possibly\n filter parameters, like in the normal --vf syntax.\n
  • Add a vf toggle @deband to input.conf. This toggles the\n "disabled" flag for the filter with the label deband when\n the a key is hit.
    • \n\n
    \n
    \n\n
    \n
    vf-command <label>\n <command> <argument> [<target>]
    \n
    Send a command to the filter. Note that currently, this only works with\n the lavfi filter. Refer to the libavfilter documentation for the\n list of supported commands for each filter.\n

    <label> is a mpv filter label, use all to\n send it to all filters at once.

    \n

    <command> and <argument> are\n filter-specific strings.

    \n

    <target> is a filter or filter instance name and\n defaults to all. Note that the target is an additional specifier\n for filters that support them, such as complex lavfi filter\n chains.

    \n
    \n
    af-command <label>\n <command> <argument> [<target>]
    \n
    Same as vf-command, but for audio filters.
    \n
    \n\n
    \n
    \n

    Miscellaneous Commands

    \n
    \n
    \n
    ignore
    \n
    Use this to "block" keys that should be unbound, and do nothing.\n Useful for disabling default bindings, without disabling all bindings with\n --input-default-bindings=no.
    \n
    drop-buffers
    \n
    Drop audio/video/demuxer buffers, and restart from fresh. Might help with\n unseekable streams that are going out of sync. This command might be\n changed or removed in the future.
    \n
    dump-cache\n <start> <end> <filename>
    \n
    Dump the current cache to the given filename. The <filename>\n file is overwritten if it already exists. <start> and\n <end> give the time range of what to dump. If no data is\n cached at the given time range, nothing may be dumped (creating a file\n with no packets).\n

    Dumping a larger part of the cache will freeze the player. No\n effort was made to fix this, as this feature was meant mostly for\n creating small excerpts.

    \n

    See --stream-record for various caveats that mostly\n apply to this command too, as both use the same underlying code for\n writing the output file.

    \n

    If <filename> is an empty string, an ongoing\n dump-cache is stopped.

    \n

    If <end> is no, then continuous dumping is\n enabled. Then, after dumping the existing parts of the cache, anything\n read from network is appended to the cache as well. This behaves similar\n to --stream-record (although it does not conflict with that\n option, and they can be both active at the same time).

    \n

    If the <end> time is after the cache, the command\n will _not_ wait and write newly received data to it.

    \n

    The end of the resulting file may be slightly damaged or\n incomplete at the end. (Not enough effort was made to ensure that the\n end lines up properly.)

    \n

    Note that this command will finish only once dumping ends.\n That means it works similar to the screenshot command, just that\n it can block much longer. If continuous dumping is used, the command\n will not finish until playback is stopped, an error happens, another\n dump-cache command is run, or an API like\n mp.abort_async_command was called to explicitly stop the command.\n See Synchronous vs. Asynchronous.

    \n

    NOTE:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \nThis was mostly created for network streams. For local\n files, there may be much better methods to create excerpts and such. There are\n tons of much more user-friendly Lua scripts, that will re-encode parts of a\n file by spawning a separate instance of ffmpeg. With network streams, \n this is not that easily possible, as the stream would have to be downloaded\n again. Even if --stream-record is used to record the stream to the\n local filesystem, there may be problems, because the recorded file is still\n written to.
    \n
    \n

    This command is experimental, and all details about it may change\n in the future.

    \n
    \n
    ab-loop
    \n
    Cycle through A-B loop states. The first command will set the A\n point (the ab-loop-a property); the second the B point, and\n the third will clear both points.
    \n
    ab-loop-dump-cache\n <filename>
    \n
    Essentially calls dump-cache with the current AB-loop points as\n arguments. Like dump-cache, this will overwrite the file at\n <filename>. Likewise, if the B point is set to no, it\n will enter continuous dumping after the existing cache was dumped.\n

    The author reserves the right to remove this command if enough\n motivation is found to move this functionality to a trivial Lua\n script.

    \n
    \n
    ab-loop-align-cache
    \n
    Re-adjust the A/B loop points to the start and end within the cache the\n ab-loop-dump-cache command will (probably) dump. Basically, it\n aligns the times on keyframes. The guess might be off especially at the\n end (due to granularity issues due to remuxing). If the cache shrinks in\n the meantime, the points set by the command will not be the effective\n parameters either.\n

    This command has an even more uncertain future than\n ab-loop-dump-cache and might disappear without replacement if the\n author decides it's useless.

    \n
    \n
    begin-vo-dragging
    \n
    Begin window dragging if supported by the current VO. This command should\n only be called while a mouse button is being pressed, otherwise it will be\n ignored. The exact effect of this command depends on the VO implementation\n of window dragging. For example, on Windows and macOS only the left mouse\n button can begin window dragging, while X11 and Wayland allow other mouse\n buttons.
    \n
    context-menu
    \n
    Show context menu on the video window. See Context Menu section for\n details.
    \n
    \n
    \n

    Undocumented commands: ao-reload\n (experimental/internal).

    \n
    \n
    \n

    List of events

    \n

    This is a partial list of events. This section describes what\n mpv_event_to_node() returns, and which is what scripting APIs and the\n JSON IPC sees. Note that the C API has separate C-level declarations with\n mpv_event, which may be slightly different.

    \n

    Note that events are asynchronous: the player core continues\n running while events are delivered to scripts and other clients. In some\n cases, you can use hooks to enforce synchronous execution.

    \n

    All events can have the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    event
    \n
    Name as the event (as returned by mpv_event_name()).
    \n
    id
    \n
    The reply_userdata field (opaque user value). If\n reply_userdata is 0, the field is not added.
    \n
    error
    \n
    Set to an error string (as returned by mpv_error_string()). This\n field is missing if no error happened, or the event type does not report\n error. Most events leave this unset.
    \n
    \n
    \n

    This list uses the event name field value, and the C API symbol in\n brackets:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    start-file\n (MPV_EVENT_START_FILE)
    \n
    Happens right before a new file is loaded. When you receive this, the\n player is loading the file (or possibly already done with it).\n

    This has the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file being loaded now.
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    end-file\n (MPV_EVENT_END_FILE)
    \n
    Happens after a file was unloaded. Typically, the player will load the\n next file right away, or quit if this was the last file.\n

    The event has the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    reason
    \n
    Has one of these values:
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    eof
    \n
    The file has ended. This can (but doesn't have to) include incomplete\n files or broken network connections under circumstances.
    \n
    stop
    \n
    Playback was ended by a command.
    \n
    quit
    \n
    Playback was ended by sending the quit command.
    \n
    error
    \n
    An error happened. In this case, an error field is present with the\n error string.
    \n
    redirect
    \n
    Happens with playlists and similar. Details see\n MPV_END_FILE_REASON_REDIRECT in the C API.
    \n
    unknown
    \n
    Unknown. Normally doesn't happen, unless the Lua API is out of sync with\n the C API. (Likewise, it could happen that your script gets reason strings\n that did not exist yet at the time your script was written.)
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file that was being played or attempted to be\n played. This has the same value as the playlist_entry_id field in\n the corresponding start-file event.
    \n
    file_error
    \n
    Set to mpv error string describing the approximate reason why playback\n failed. Unset if no error known. (In Lua scripting, this value was set on\n the error field directly. This is deprecated since mpv 0.33.0. In\n the future, this error", "before the filter name disables the filter by\n default. Everything after this is the normal filter name and possibly\n filter parameters, like in the normal --vf syntax.\n
  • Add a vf toggle @deband to input.conf. This toggles the\n "disabled" flag for the filter with the label deband when\n the a key is hit.
    • \n\n
    \n

    \n
    An error happened. In this case, an error field is present with the\n error string.
    \n
    redirect
    \n
    Happens with playlists and similar. Details see\n MPV_END_FILE_REASON_REDIRECT in the C API.
    \n
    unknown
    \n
    Unknown. Normally doesn't happen, unless the Lua API is out of sync with\n the C API. (Likewise, it could happen that your script gets reason strings\n that did not exist yet at the time your script was written.)
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file that was being played or attempted to be\n played. This has the same value as the playlist_entry_id field in\n the corresponding start-file event.
    \n
    file_error
    \n
    Set to mpv error string describing the approximate reason why playback\n failed. Unset if no error known. (In Lua scripting, this value was set on\n the error field directly. This is deprecated since mpv 0.33.0. In\n the future, this error", "before the filter name disables the filter by\n default. Everything after this is the normal filter name and possibly\n filter parameters, like in the normal --vf syntax.\n
  • Add a vf toggle @deband to input.conf. This toggles the\n "disabled" flag for the filter with the label deband when\n the a key is hit.
    • \n\n
    \n
    \n\n
    \n
    vf-command <label>\n <command> <argument> [<target>]
    \n
    Send a command to the filter. Note that currently, this only works with\n the lavfi filter. Refer to the libavfilter documentation for the\n list of supported commands for each filter.\n

    <label> is a mpv filter label, use all to\n send it to all filters at once.

    \n

    <command> and <argument> are\n filter-specific strings.

    \n

    <target> is a filter or filter instance name and\n defaults to all. Note that the target is an additional specifier\n for filters that support them, such as complex lavfi filter\n chains.

    \n
    \n
    af-command <label>\n <command> <argument> [<target>]
    \n
    Same as vf-command, but for audio filters.
    \n
    \n\n
    \n
    \n

    Miscellaneous Commands

    \n
    \n
    \n
    ignore
    \n
    Use this to "block" keys that should be unbound, and do nothing.\n Useful for disabling default bindings, without disabling all bindings with\n --input-default-bindings=no.
    \n
    drop-buffers
    \n
    Drop audio/video/demuxer buffers, and restart from fresh. Might help with\n unseekable streams that are going out of sync. This command might be\n changed or removed in the future.
    \n
    dump-cache\n <start> <end> <filename>
    \n
    Dump the current cache to the given filename. The <filename>\n file is overwritten if it already exists. <start> and\n <end> give the time range of what to dump. If no data is\n cached at the given time range, nothing may be dumped (creating a file\n with no packets).\n

    Dumping a larger part of the cache will freeze the player. No\n effort was made to fix this, as this feature was meant mostly for\n creating small excerpts.

    \n

    See --stream-record for various caveats that mostly\n apply to this command too, as both use the same underlying code for\n writing the output file.

    \n

    If <filename> is an empty string, an ongoing\n dump-cache is stopped.

    \n

    If <end> is no, then continuous dumping is\n enabled. Then, after dumping the existing parts of the cache, anything\n read from network is appended to the cache as well. This behaves similar\n to --stream-record (although it does not conflict with that\n option, and they can be both active at the same time).

    \n

    If the <end> time is after the cache, the command\n will _not_ wait and write newly received data to it.

    \n

    The end of the resulting file may be slightly damaged or\n incomplete at the end. (Not enough effort was made to ensure that the\n end lines up properly.)

    \n

    Note that this command will finish only once dumping ends.\n That means it works similar to the screenshot command, just that\n it can block much longer. If continuous dumping is used, the command\n will not finish until playback is stopped, an error happens, another\n dump-cache command is run, or an API like\n mp.abort_async_command was called to explicitly stop the command.\n See Synchronous vs. Asynchronous.

    \n

    NOTE:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \nThis was mostly created for network streams. For local\n files, there may be much better methods to create excerpts and such. There are\n tons of much more user-friendly Lua scripts, that will re-encode parts of a\n file by spawning a separate instance of ffmpeg. With network streams, \n this is not that easily possible, as the stream would have to be downloaded\n again. Even if --stream-record is used to record the stream to the\n local filesystem, there may be problems, because the recorded file is still\n written to.
    \n
    \n

    This command is experimental, and all details about it may change\n in the future.

    \n
    \n
    ab-loop
    \n
    Cycle through A-B loop states. The first command will set the A\n point (the ab-loop-a property); the second the B point, and\n the third will clear both points.
    \n
    ab-loop-dump-cache\n <filename>
    \n
    Essentially calls dump-cache with the current AB-loop points as\n arguments. Like dump-cache, this will overwrite the file at\n <filename>. Likewise, if the B point is set to no, it\n will enter continuous dumping after the existing cache was dumped.\n

    The author reserves the right to remove this command if enough\n motivation is found to move this functionality to a trivial Lua\n script.

    \n
    \n
    ab-loop-align-cache
    \n
    Re-adjust the A/B loop points to the start and end within the cache the\n ab-loop-dump-cache command will (probably) dump. Basically, it\n aligns the times on keyframes. The guess might be off especially at the\n end (due to granularity issues due to remuxing). If the cache shrinks in\n the meantime, the points set by the command will not be the effective\n parameters either.\n

    This command has an even more uncertain future than\n ab-loop-dump-cache and might disappear without replacement if the\n author decides it's useless.

    \n
    \n
    begin-vo-dragging
    \n
    Begin window dragging if supported by the current VO. This command should\n only be called while a mouse button is being pressed, otherwise it will be\n ignored. The exact effect of this command depends on the VO implementation\n of window dragging. For example, on Windows and macOS only the left mouse\n button can begin window dragging, while X11 and Wayland allow other mouse\n buttons.
    \n
    context-menu
    \n
    Show context menu on the video window. See Context Menu section for\n details.
    \n
    \n
    \n

    Undocumented commands: ao-reload\n (experimental/internal).

    \n
    \n
    \n

    List of events

    \n

    This is a partial list of events. This section describes what\n mpv_event_to_node() returns, and which is what scripting APIs and the\n JSON IPC sees. Note that the C API has separate C-level declarations with\n mpv_event, which may be slightly different.

    \n

    Note that events are asynchronous: the player core continues\n running while events are delivered to scripts and other clients. In some\n cases, you can use hooks to enforce synchronous execution.

    \n

    All events can have the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    event
    \n
    Name as the event (as returned by mpv_event_name()).
    \n
    id
    \n
    The reply_userdata field (opaque user value). If\n reply_userdata is 0, the field is not added.
    \n
    error
    \n
    Set to an error string (as returned by mpv_error_string()). This\n field is missing if no error happened, or the event type does not report\n error. Most events leave this unset.
    \n
    \n
    \n

    This list uses the event name field value, and the C API symbol in\n brackets:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    start-file\n (MPV_EVENT_START_FILE)
    \n
    Happens right before a new file is loaded. When you receive this, the\n player is loading the file (or possibly already done with it).\n

    This has the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file being loaded now.
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    end-file\n (MPV_EVENT_END_FILE)
    \n
    Happens after a file was unloaded. Typically, the player will load the\n next file right away, or quit if this was the last file.\n

    The event has the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    reason
    \n
    Has one of these values:
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    eof
    \n
    The file has ended. This can (but doesn't have to) include incomplete\n files or broken network connections under circumstances.
    \n
    stop
    \n
    Playback was ended by a command.
    \n
    quit
    \n
    Playback was ended by sending the quit command.
    \n
    error
    \n
    An error happened. In this case, an error field is present with the\n error string.
    \n
    redirect
    \n
    Happens with playlists and similar. Details see\n MPV_END_FILE_REASON_REDIRECT in the C API.
    \n
    unknown
    \n
    Unknown. Normally doesn't happen, unless the Lua API is out of sync with\n the C API. (Likewise, it could happen that your script gets reason strings\n that did not exist yet at the time your script was written.)
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file that was being played or attempted to be\n played. This has the same value as the playlist_entry_id field in\n the corresponding start-file event.
    \n
    file_error
    \n
    Set to mpv error string describing the approximate reason why playback\n failed. Unset if no error known. (In Lua scripting, this value was set on\n the error field directly. This is deprecated since mpv 0.33.0. In\n the future, this error", "before the filter name disables the filter by\n default. Everything after this is the normal filter name and possibly\n filter parameters, like in the normal --vf syntax.\n
  • Add a vf toggle @deband to input.conf. This toggles the\n "disabled" flag for the filter with the label deband when\n the a key is hit.
    • \n\n
    \n
    \n\n

    \n
    stop
    \n
    Playback was ended by a command.
    \n
    quit
    \n
    Playback was ended by sending the quit command.
    \n
    error
    \n
    An error happened. In this case, an error field is present with the\n error string.
    \n
    redirect
    \n
    Happens with playlists and similar. Details see\n MPV_END_FILE_REASON_REDIRECT in the C API.
    \n
    unknown
    \n
    Unknown. Normally doesn't happen, unless the Lua API is out of sync with\n the C API. (Likewise, it could happen that your script gets reason strings\n that did not exist yet at the time your script was written.)
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file that was being played or attempted to be\n played. This has the same value as the playlist_entry_id field in\n the corresponding start-file event.
    \n
    file_error
    \n
    Set to mpv error string describing the approximate reason why playback\n failed. Unset if no error known. (In Lua scripting, this value was set on\n the error field directly. This is deprecated since mpv 0.33.0. In\n the future, this error", "before the filter name disables the filter by\n default. Everything after this is the normal filter name and possibly\n filter parameters, like in the normal --vf syntax.\n
  • Add a vf toggle @deband to input.conf. This toggles the\n "disabled" flag for the filter with the label deband when\n the a key is hit.
    • \n\n
    \n
    \n\n
    \n
    vf-command <label>\n <command> <argument> [<target>]
    \n
    Send a command to the filter. Note that currently, this only works with\n the lavfi filter. Refer to the libavfilter documentation for the\n list of supported commands for each filter.\n

    <label> is a mpv filter label, use all to\n send it to all filters at once.

    \n

    <command> and <argument> are\n filter-specific strings.

    \n

    <target> is a filter or filter instance name and\n defaults to all. Note that the target is an additional specifier\n for filters that support them, such as complex lavfi filter\n chains.

    \n
    \n
    af-command <label>\n <command> <argument> [<target>]
    \n
    Same as vf-command, but for audio filters.
    \n
    \n\n
    \n
    \n

    Miscellaneous Commands

    \n
    \n
    \n
    ignore
    \n
    Use this to "block" keys that should be unbound, and do nothing.\n Useful for disabling default bindings, without disabling all bindings with\n --input-default-bindings=no.
    \n
    drop-buffers
    \n
    Drop audio/video/demuxer buffers, and restart from fresh. Might help with\n unseekable streams that are going out of sync. This command might be\n changed or removed in the future.
    \n
    dump-cache\n <start> <end> <filename>
    \n
    Dump the current cache to the given filename. The <filename>\n file is overwritten if it already exists. <start> and\n <end> give the time range of what to dump. If no data is\n cached at the given time range, nothing may be dumped (creating a file\n with no packets).\n

    Dumping a larger part of the cache will freeze the player. No\n effort was made to fix this, as this feature was meant mostly for\n creating small excerpts.

    \n

    See --stream-record for various caveats that mostly\n apply to this command too, as both use the same underlying code for\n writing the output file.

    \n

    If <filename> is an empty string, an ongoing\n dump-cache is stopped.

    \n

    If <end> is no, then continuous dumping is\n enabled. Then, after dumping the existing parts of the cache, anything\n read from network is appended to the cache as well. This behaves similar\n to --stream-record (although it does not conflict with that\n option, and they can be both active at the same time).

    \n

    If the <end> time is after the cache, the command\n will _not_ wait and write newly received data to it.

    \n

    The end of the resulting file may be slightly damaged or\n incomplete at the end. (Not enough effort was made to ensure that the\n end lines up properly.)

    \n

    Note that this command will finish only once dumping ends.\n That means it works similar to the screenshot command, just that\n it can block much longer. If continuous dumping is used, the command\n will not finish until playback is stopped, an error happens, another\n dump-cache command is run, or an API like\n mp.abort_async_command was called to explicitly stop the command.\n See Synchronous vs. Asynchronous.

    \n

    NOTE:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \nThis was mostly created for network streams. For local\n files, there may be much better methods to create excerpts and such. There are\n tons of much more user-friendly Lua scripts, that will re-encode parts of a\n file by spawning a separate instance of ffmpeg. With network streams, \n this is not that easily possible, as the stream would have to be downloaded\n again. Even if --stream-record is used to record the stream to the\n local filesystem, there may be problems, because the recorded file is still\n written to.
    \n
    \n

    This command is experimental, and all details about it may change\n in the future.

    \n
    \n
    ab-loop
    \n
    Cycle through A-B loop states. The first command will set the A\n point (the ab-loop-a property); the second the B point, and\n the third will clear both points.
    \n
    ab-loop-dump-cache\n <filename>
    \n
    Essentially calls dump-cache with the current AB-loop points as\n arguments. Like dump-cache, this will overwrite the file at\n <filename>. Likewise, if the B point is set to no, it\n will enter continuous dumping after the existing cache was dumped.\n

    The author reserves the right to remove this command if enough\n motivation is found to move this functionality to a trivial Lua\n script.

    \n
    \n
    ab-loop-align-cache
    \n
    Re-adjust the A/B loop points to the start and end within the cache the\n ab-loop-dump-cache command will (probably) dump. Basically, it\n aligns the times on keyframes. The guess might be off especially at the\n end (due to granularity issues due to remuxing). If the cache shrinks in\n the meantime, the points set by the command will not be the effective\n parameters either.\n

    This command has an even more uncertain future than\n ab-loop-dump-cache and might disappear without replacement if the\n author decides it's useless.

    \n
    \n
    begin-vo-dragging
    \n
    Begin window dragging if supported by the current VO. This command should\n only be called while a mouse button is being pressed, otherwise it will be\n ignored. The exact effect of this command depends on the VO implementation\n of window dragging. For example, on Windows and macOS only the left mouse\n button can begin window dragging, while X11 and Wayland allow other mouse\n buttons.
    \n
    context-menu
    \n
    Show context menu on the video window. See Context Menu section for\n details.
    \n
    \n
    \n

    Undocumented commands: ao-reload\n (experimental/internal).

    \n
    \n
    \n

    List of events

    \n

    This is a partial list of events. This section describes what\n mpv_event_to_node() returns, and which is what scripting APIs and the\n JSON IPC sees. Note that the C API has separate C-level declarations with\n mpv_event, which may be slightly different.

    \n

    Note that events are asynchronous: the player core continues\n running while events are delivered to scripts and other clients. In some\n cases, you can use hooks to enforce synchronous execution.

    \n

    All events can have the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    event
    \n
    Name as the event (as returned by mpv_event_name()).
    \n
    id
    \n
    The reply_userdata field (opaque user value). If\n reply_userdata is 0, the field is not added.
    \n
    error
    \n
    Set to an error string (as returned by mpv_error_string()). This\n field is missing if no error happened, or the event type does not report\n error. Most events leave this unset.
    \n
    \n
    \n

    This list uses the event name field value, and the C API symbol in\n brackets:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    start-file\n (MPV_EVENT_START_FILE)
    \n
    Happens right before a new file is loaded. When you receive this, the\n player is loading the file (or possibly already done with it).\n

    This has the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file being loaded now.
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    end-file\n (MPV_EVENT_END_FILE)
    \n
    Happens after a file was unloaded. Typically, the player will load the\n next file right away, or quit if this was the last file.\n

    The event has the following fields:

    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    reason
    \n
    Has one of these values:
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    eof
    \n
    The file has ended. This can (but doesn't have to) include incomplete\n files or broken network connections under circumstances.
    \n
    stop
    \n
    Playback was ended by a command.
    \n
    quit
    \n
    Playback was ended by sending the quit command.
    \n
    error
    \n
    An error happened. In this case, an error field is present with the\n error string.
    \n
    redirect
    \n
    Happens with playlists and similar. Details see\n MPV_END_FILE_REASON_REDIRECT in the C API.
    \n
    unknown
    \n
    Unknown. Normally doesn't happen, unless the Lua API is out of sync with\n the C API. (Likewise, it could happen that your script gets reason strings\n that did not exist yet at the time your script was written.)
    \n
    \n
    \n
    \n
    playlist_entry_id
    \n
    Playlist entry ID of the file that was being played or attempted to be\n played. This has the same value as the playlist_entry_id field in\n the corresponding start-file event.
    \n
    file_error
    \n
    Set to mpv error string describing the approximate reason why playback\n failed. Unset if no error known. (In Lua scripting, this value was set on\n the error field directly. This is deprecated since mpv 0.33.0. In\n the future, this error", "before the filter name disables the filter by\n default. Everything after this is the normal filter name and possibly\n filter parameters, like in the normal --vf syntax.\n
  • Add a vf toggle @deband to input.conf. This toggles the\n "disabled" flag for the filter with the label deband when\n the a key is hit.
    • \n\n
    \n
